The "2pl-full-pipeline-coverage" name likely refers to comprehensive testing across the full 2PL pipeline.

Looking at the recent completions, I see `exp006-s1only-gsm8k-2pl` finished at 09:55 after the D041 bug fix, along with several seed=123 expansion experiments. I need to verify whether the 2PL experiment ran correctly post-fix and understand what analysis the worker actually produced.

Checking the 2PL results: the gamma estimates show no significant effect (CI crosses zero), convergence diagnostics look solid with max Rhat near 1.0 and good effective sample sizes, and the posterior predictive check is acceptable. This confirms the corrected 2PL run executed properly. Now I'm examining the seed=123 eval metrics to ensure they're consistent and reasonable, plus auditing GPU resource usage and identifying any coverage gaps. Looking at the rest of the benchmark results, GSM8K shows an unexpected dip for the 1.5B d100 variant while other metrics improve substantially—HumanEval jumps by nearly 11 percentage points. This GSM8K regression is puzzling, but I recall from the roadmap that the project is already tracking several known issues including coverage problems and negative gamma values on ARC. Let me check what experiments are stuck in PREPARING state that should've been running.

The base_pretrained evals for both model sizes are actively running on GPUs 2 and 4 and have been going for about 2 hours now. Meanwhile, both SFT variants with d005 and d025 seeds failed with the same shell syntax error, which suggests a systematic issue in how those experiments were configured. Since all 6 RTX 4090D GPUs are allocated to this project, there's no resource contention to worry about. I should also verify whether the 15B SFT d005 failure is part of the same pattern.

Looking at that 15B experiment, it crashed almost immediately after starting—only 25 seconds in with EXIT_CODE=2—and the command explicitly specifies `--gpu 0`, which points to that dead code issue flagged in the roadmap as critical. This is definitely the D041 task that needs addressing.

Now reviewing my analysis: the GSM8K rerun with proper methodology shows solid convergence metrics and no statistical issues with the gamma parameter, which aligns with what we've seen across the broader dataset. The seed=123 evaluation results are still coming through.

For GPU allocation, I'm checking whether the two evaluation processes running on GPUs 2 and 4 have proper CUDA device visibility set, since all 6 GPUs are committed to this project.  The command lines don't explicitly show CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES, though the processes did land on separate GPUs, suggesting it may have been configured in the environment. The registry entries for both evaluations are stuck in PREPARING state without visible command specs, indicating a tracking issue between the actual running processes and the system registry.

The SFT experiment failed after 25 seconds with EXIT_CODE=2, likely due to the --gpu flag dead code mentioned in the roadmap. Looking at the broader picture, there's a significant mismatch between the formal experiment plan and what's actually running—several planned experiments like the signal pipeline and RRF aggregation remain in planning stages while ad-hoc variants are executing separately. The most pressing concern is that two SFT experiments needed for the seed=123 pooled IRT expansion have both failed with the same exit code, and while this is a known roadmap issue, it's actively blocking progress.

On the positive side, the exp006 GSM8K experiment converged well after the D041 fix, and the 0.5B and 1.5B seed=123 evals finished successfully. However, there are tracking issues with the exp_008 base pretrained evals—they've been running for over 2 hours on GPUs 2 and 4 but the registry still shows them as PREPARING, and the process commands don't explicitly show CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES assignments, which could violate the C004 constraint. GPU allocation itself is fine since all 6 GPUs are properly assigned to the project.

Looking at the 1.5B d100 results, the GSM8K difference compared to d000 is only -0.91pp, which falls well within expected cross-seed variance based on the ROADMAP benchmarks. The worker "2pl-full-pipeline-coverage" likely completed the seed=123 evals, ran the 2PL pipeline analysis, and computed coverage metrics around the 10:40:45 timestamp.

- **Overall**: 1/13 done (7.7%)

This is extremely low! BUT - this is the formal experiment_plan.yaml coverage which doesn't reflect the many ad-hoc experiments (exp006-*, exp002-*, exp008-*) that have been running. 

The real issue is that the plan IDs aren't actually linked to the registry experiments. For instance, exp-002 is marked as planned in the formal plan, but the registry shows multiple signal experiments are already done. Same with exp-008 and exp-006 - they're marked planned but have actual completed variants in the registry. This is a disconnect between the plan documentation and what's actually been executed, not a genuine coverage gap.

### GPU Status
- GPUs 0,1,3,5: idle
- GPU 2: 0.5B base pretrained eval
- GPU 4: 1.5B base pretrained eval
- All 6 GPUs allocated to project - no boundary violation

### Running Processes C004 Compliance
I couldn't verify CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES through /proc due to pipe restrictions, but the processes are running on separate GPUs (2 and 4) without explicit CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES in the command line, which might violate the C004 constraint requiring explicit GPU assignment in the command.

### Key Experiment Findings

**exp006-s1only-gsm8k-2pl** (after D041 bug fix):
- Good convergence: rhat=1.0024, ESS=3604

, no divergences
- Gamma=0.1389, CI=[-0.306, 0.591] - NOT significant, PPP=0.2835 - acceptable
- The GSM8K S1-only signal doesn't produce significant contamination even with the simpler 2PL model, whereas the oracle shows gamma=3.77 with significance, suggesting signal quality is the limiting factor rather than model complexity

**Seed=123 eval results** (newly completed):
- 1.5B d000 s123: mmlu=0.5855, gsm8k=0.6535, arc=0.7577
- 1.5B d100 s123: mmlu=0.5956, gsm8k=0.6444, arc=0.7816
- 0.5B d000 s123 results coming in

, showing contamination effects of +1pp on MMLU, -0.9pp on GSM8K, and +2.4pp on ARC for the 1.5B model, though the GSM8K dip looks like noise

The intermediate dosage experiments for 1.5B (d005 and d025) both hit the --gpu dead code issue and failed immediately, blocking further seed=123 expansion until that's resolved.

Looking at the broader experiment status, exp_004_sim_sweep shows as done but failed, though there's a separate v5 variant. The worker appears to have monitored the seed=123 eval completions, kicked off exp008 base pretrained evals on GPU 2/4, and possibly ran some 2PL pipeline analysis. There are a couple of compliance issues to flag: the exp_008 processes are running on the correct GPUs but don't have explicit CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES set in their command lines, and the registry tracking for those experiments still needs updating.

The exp_001_sft_15B variants failed due to a known --gpu dead code issue that's blocking seed=123 expansion. There's also a disconnect between the formal plan showing 7.7% coverage and the many ad-hoc experiments running outside it. On the positive side, exp006 with the D041 fix shows good convergence with non-significant gamma, suggesting signal quality rather than model complexity is the limiting factor. Let me dig into the C004 compliance issue more closely by examining what those process command lines actually show. I can't verify the environment variables due to the pipe restriction, so I'll flag this as a potential issue while acknowledging the limitation. Looking at resource utilization, four out of six GPUs are sitting idle, which is concerning with only ten days until the deadline. The exp_001_eval_3B model shows the expected GSM8K collapse that's already been documented. Now I need to assess whether the worker actually completed the "2pl-full-pipeline-coverage" task as intended, which should have involved running the full pipeline.

The 2PL experiment ran successfully and seed=123 evaluations expanded coverage, with exp008 base evals launched, but the 1.5B SFT jobs for d005 and d025 failed, so the pipeline coverage wasn't fully realized despite the progress made.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a clearer picture of what the "full-gpu-saturation-6tasks" worker did:

**GPU Status:**
- GPU 0: 100%, 14901 MiB - PID 549516 - 1.5B d025 s123 SFT (NEW, running 1h50m)
- GPU 1: 41%, 15961 MiB - PID 549858 - 1.5B d005 s123 SFT (NEW, running 1h19m)


- GPU 2: 0% util, 8225 MiB - PID 530893 - 0.5B base pretrained eval (continuing)
- GPU 3: IDLE
- GPU 4: 54%, 5683 MiB - PID 530975 - 1.5B base pretrained eval (continuing)
- GPU 5: IDLE

The worker successfully resolved the `--gpu` dead code issue from the previous failure, and the new SFT commands for d005 and d025 are now properly configured.

However, the SFT processes running on GPUs 0 and 1 still lack explicit CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES in their command line arguments, which could still violate the C004 constraint. The new experiments are being registered with their expected naming convention.

The registry shows both new SFT experiments as PENDING even though they've been running for nearly two hours, indicating a tracking mismatch. GPUs 3 and 5 remain idle while the downstream evaluation tasks wait for the SFT jobs to complete, so there's potential underutilization of the available compute resources.

Now I'm realizing the worker removed the `--gpu` flag to fix the argparse error, but that raises a question about how each process knows which GPU to use. If the script defaults to cuda:0, then the process on GPU 1 shouldn't be there unless CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES is set in the environment beforehand—which would make that GPU appear as cuda:0 internally to the process. However, constraint C004 explicitly requires CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES to be visible in the command line itself, not just in the environment.

Looking at the eval commands, there's inconsistency: the 3B eval shows `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=4` explicitly, but the d000 s123 eval doesn't. So I need to flag both the registry status still being PENDING and the missing explicit CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES in the SFT command as concerns, while acknowledging the worker did fix the dead code issue and got the training running.

On the GPU front, I'm seeing 4 of 6 active with 2 waiting on SFT completion, but GPU 2 is puzzling—it's got 8GB allocated and a process running for hours yet showing 0% utilization, which suggests it's either stuck in CPU-bound work like data loading or the process has stalled entirely.
```

2. **exp_001_sft_15B_d025_s123_v2**: Also now RUNNING. Registry shows the same pattern - CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 is set, PID 549516 on cuda:0, and it's a retry after the original process died. So the registry spec.command definitely includes CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES, which contradicts what I was seeing in the ps output earlier.

Actually, I realize the issue - when you prefix a command with environment variables like `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 python script.py`, the ps output only shows the executable and arguments, not the environment variable prefix. The env var gets set in the process environment but doesn't appear in the command line itself. So C004 is satisfied here since the registered command includes CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES, even though ps doesn't show it.

For the exp_008 base pretrained evals though, the registry only has gpu_id without a spec.command field, so I can't verify C004 for those - but that's another worker's concern.

This worker successfully fixed the argparse error, added proper CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES handling, resubmitted the experiments on the correct GPUs, updated the registry status, and pre-registered the downstream evals. It's a solid resolution to the previous failure.

Looking at the current GPU allocation, I'm seeing the two resubmitted SFT jobs running hot on GPUs 0 and 1, but GPU 2 is concerning - it's allocated 8GB for the 0.5B base eval but showing zero utilization after running for over 3 hours, which suggests it might be stuck.

I checked for eval logs but they're not where I expected. However, I should keep in mind that GPU 2 belongs to a different worker's experiment, so while it's worth flagging, my main focus here is on the SFT retry status. Let me pull together a combined report covering both the saturation metrics and the retry progress.

1. The processes might be logging to stdout/stderr in their tmux sessions


2. Or they're running without any log capture at all

Looking at GPU utilization gives me a clearer picture though. GPU 0 is maxed out at 100% with 14.9GB memory, which strongly indicates the SFT training is actively running. GPU 1 is at 41% with 16GB allocated, suggesting it might be training but hitting some I/O bottleneck. The eval on GPU 2 is concerning though—it's showing 0% utilization after 3+ hours despite having 8.2GB of memory allocated, which doesn't look right.

But wait, the eval command includes HumanEval, which is code execution and would be CPU-bound rather than GPU-bound. That could explain the zero GPU utilization. The process started around 08:33 UTC and it's now roughly 11:10 UTC, so it's been running for about 2.5 hours. For a 0.5B model, that timing seems plausible based on previous eval runs that took 2.5-3 hours.

The empty output directory is still concerning though, even if the runtime is within expected range. Let me assess what's actually happening here.

For the new SFT runs, GPU 0 is maxed out at 100% with 14.9GB and GPU 1 is at 41% with 16GB allocated - both are actively training and have been for about 2 hours without any checkpoints saved yet, which is typical for LoRA SFT since checkpoints usually get saved at the end or at specific intervals.

Now I should write up the review report summarizing what the worker accomplished.

The worker fixed the dead `--gpu` flag issue in the SFT commands, kicked off two new 1.5B SFT v2 runs (d005 and d025 on different GPUs), pre-registered the downstream eval experiments as PREPARING, and updated the base pretrained evals to RUNNING.

However, there are some issues to flag: the SFT v2 experiments are still showing as PENDING in the registry despite having active processes, the new SFT commands aren't exposing CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES in the process list, and only 4 out of 6 GPUs are actually being used (GPUs 3 and 5 are idle). GPU 2 is sitting at 0% utilization with 8GB allocated and an empty output directory for the 0.5B base eval after 3 hours - it's probably stuck in a CPU-bound phase like HumanEval but I should keep an eye on it. The SFT checkpoint directories exist but are empty, which is likely fine since checkpoints get saved at training completion. I'm also noticing there are no log files for the new SFT v2 runs or base pretrained evals in the runs directory yet.

There's a traceability gap here - I need to figure out how to better track what's actually running versus what the registry thinks is running.
